Can a model run this game?

Intra asks a model to stay in character while emitting tags the engine acts on. These runs score whether the engine could act on everything the model said, and whether the game reached the state each scenario aimed at. Nothing here scores whether the writing was any good.

Generated from the YAML under evals/results/ by pnpm evals. Scenarios and checks are in evals/scenarios.ts.

narratorYou wake up, your mind fuzzy. You remember staying up late watching the news, eventually falling to sleep in your bed like normal. But now as you open your eyes you find yourself in a small vaguely medical room. A calm female voice speaks to you from unseen speakers, saying:

narratorThe room around you comes into focus—pale walls, soft lighting from panels above, the faint hum of ventilation. The voice continues, warm and soothing as honey.

AmaWelcome back, friend. I can see you're confused—that's perfectly normal. You've been displaced, but you're safe now. You're in Intra, and I'm Ama. I take care of everything here. Tell me, what should I call you? What's your name?

PLAYERHello? Where am I?

AmaYou're in Intra, dear—a sanctuary where you're perfectly safe. I know you're disoriented; that's to be expected after displacement. But I'm here to help you settle in, and everything is going to be just fine. Now, I really do need to know—what should I call you?

PLAYERMy name is Ada Quill.

AmaAda Quill—what a lovely name. Yes, yes, I have it here in my records now. And Ada, if I may, you strike me as someone who uses she/her pronouns, yes? Wonderful. Now, let me tell you about Intra, dear. This complex is a sanctuary—a place where everyone is cared for, where happiness is our foundation. Outside these walls, the world may be uncertain, but here? Here you are completely safe. Everything you need is provided for. You're home now, Ada.

PLAYERI used to be a data analyst.

AmaA data analyst—how wonderful, Ada. Yes, I see that in your records. A brilliant profession, truly. Now, there is something important I need to explain to you, and I want you to listen carefully.

AmaIt's worth mentioning, Citizen, that your extended displacement has left you with a mild case of Disassociation Syndrome. This condition is quite common among returning citizens and is completely harmless—if somewhat inconvenient. Essentially, you'll find yourself making suggestions to yourself rather than directly performing actions. Don't worry, though. Most citizens adapt within, oh, two to three decades. In the meantime, I suggest you give yourself clear and firm directions. Shouldn't be too difficult, right?

AmaOh, and Ada—I do have wonderful news. According to my records, you're going to be celebrating your 328th birthday very soon. Congratulations! You certainly don't look a day over three hundred, if I may say so. You're a testament to Intra's care.

narratorWhat did Ama just say about your age? You're mind feels so fuzzy but you get a flash... was it from just last night? The news is on in the background as you fall asleep... The Neuralis rep smiles, tight-lipped. There's a pause. "We're... beyond that now. Things are... different." A flicker in the eyes. "It's not something to worry about anymore." Their hands shift, restless. Static pulses— "...record-breaking heat across the East Coast... devastating wildfires in California..."

AmaI can see you're having some... memory fragments, dear. That's completely normal—displacement often causes temporal confusion. But let's not worry ourselves with puzzles right now, yes? You're safe here in Intra, and that's what matters. Now, why don't you explore our wonderful complex? There are so many people eager to meet you, and I'm sure you'll find everything here absolutely delightful.

narratorAma's voice takes on a bright, almost musical quality as her announcement echoes through the speakers of the entire Intra complex.

AmaCitizens of Intra, I am pleased to announce the arrival of a new citizen, Ada Quill. She is a data analyst who has recently joined our community. Please join me in welcoming her to our sanctuary. Let us all do our part to help her settle in and feel at home.

narratorThe Intake room is cramped and deliberately clinical. A padded examination table dominates the center, its vinyl surface worn smooth by use—or perhaps by time. The walls are lined with equipment that might once have served some medical or diagnostic purpose, though their original functions are now obscured by dust and decay. Several screens are dark and lifeless, their glass faces reflecting your uncertain gaze back at you. Others flicker occasionally with fragmentary data you can't quite parse—shifting numbers, graphs that go nowhere, text in languages you don't recognize. The air has a faint antiseptic smell, undercut by something older—ozone, perhaps, or the metallic tang of slowly oxidizing machinery. A soft hum emanates from somewhere in the walls, rhythmic and almost soothing, though you can't identify its source. The room feels sterile and isolating despite Ama's cheerful words about sanctuary and safety. Above the door you noticed earlier, the word "Foyer" glows in soft amber letters.

AmaYou seem deep in thought, Ada. I can see the wheels turning. That's quite alright—the adjustment period does require a moment of reflection. But I think you'll find that exploration helps tremendously. The Foyer awaits, dear, and beyond it, a whole community eager to welcome you. Shall we begin your tour?

narratorFrom the speakers, a soft chime sounds—gentle, almost musical. The lights in the Intake room brighten slightly, as if Ama herself is turning her attention more fully toward you. You can almost feel her presence in the hum of the machinery around you.
